An owner-side breakage of 017's page alone therefore shows as the route's bounded 503s, not as readiness. A full-path probe needs a minted probe session (a write) or a readiness routine in a migration; neither was in that stage (no 019), and 019 and 020 add none. *Amended (commit-2 review, CX-C1/R-E):* a second, non-mutating probe, `probeSessionMint`, calls `tmpos_mint.m7a_session_create` with every argument NULL and requires exactly `invalid`, which 018 answers before any read or write; startup step 10 and readiness add it as `session_mint`. It proves the login's EXECUTE on the mint routine and the routine's presence — not its proof, standing or insert paths, the same shape of gap this entry already records for the customer probe | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P2 | Accepted for this stage; G-OBSERVE |
| DA-109 | **Database tests could fall back to an ambient database; closed in the test harness, with two residuals.** In M7A-CUSTOMERS-P2 a CI-order replay gave `npm run test:db` a socket-form `TM_POS_TEST_DATABASE_URL`. The suites stripped its `?host=` and re-supplied the socket only for a cluster they had started themselves, so postgres.js filled host, port, user and password from the ambient `PG*` settings and authenticated to a managed server (it stopped at database selection; no SQL ran). M7A-DBTEST-ISOLATION-P0 resolves one target per suite in `tests/db/localPostgres.harness.mjs` (`resolveDisposableTarget`): the supplied DSN is validated and never repaired (a socket directory strictly below the temp root, in normal form, and still below it once the symlinks of its nearest existing ancestor resolve — a dangling link is refused; no port; unknown DSN parameters refused), and otherwise a fresh socket-only cluster is started. Every client is built by `testClient` / `testDriver` / `testDriverVia` / `checkedDriver` with host, port, role, database and password explicit (the password a function, so an empty one cannot fall back), and each refuses while any ambient database setting is present. A driver call must name its role: a socket URL has no host, so a username set on it is silently dropped. Six converted call sites (productionEntry 2, productionEntryPreflight 2, tenantAdmission 1, tenantDirectory 1) had lost their explicit role and ran as the target's owner; the first final run failed M6PF-01 (least-privilege preflight) and M5T-10 (revoked grant → 503), while productionEntry and tenantAdmission passed with those clients as the owner. The role is explicit again at every site, and `driverOver` refuses a call that names none (DBISO-4). The harness removes every `PG*`, `…DATABASE…` and `…DB_URL` variable from its process on load; `scripts/run-db-tests.mjs` (now `npm run test:db` and every CI database step) and every suite subprocess get an allowlisted environment (`childEnv`). The test executor (`createPostgresExecutor`) now passes port and password explicitly and refuses a DSN that names no role. Guarded by `tests/quality/db-test-ambient-fallback.test.mjs` (runner) and `db-test-ambient-fallback.suites.mjs` (its own CI step): a synthetic ambient configuration points at a listener the test owns, and the entry code dialed it 21/2/2 times (migrationEngine / rlsIsolation / auditAtomicity) and 28 times through `npm run test:db`; the fix dials it 0 times. **Residuals:** (1) a TCP target is accepted only at `127.0.0.1` with an explicit port, on GitHub Actions, when the step declares `TM_POS_TEST_DATABASE_KIND=ci-disposable-service` — a deliberate, non-ambient declaration, but the client cannot prove the listener behind it is the job's disposable service; (2) `scripts/supabase-migrate.ts --status` reaches the executor without the harness, so a supplied socket directory there is checked lexically (normal form, strictly below the temp root) but not through symlinks; (3) `checkedDriver` (only the driver-defect probe child) accepts the probe's own `socket` connection hook without a transport check, by design — its other fields stay explicit and ambient settings are still refused | M7A-DBTEST-ISOLATION-P0 | Closed for ambient fallback; residuals (1)–(3) accepted for test infrastructure; G-MIGRATE |
| DA-110 | **The driver's type fetch can reject unhandled when a fresh pool is retired, and the runtime treats that as fatal.** CI run 36354123120 (attempt 1, `9a25cb7`) failed M6R-PERF (`tests/db/rateLimiter.integration.test.mjs`) with `unhandledRejection write CONNECTION_DESTROYED`. The same signature failed attempt 1 of CI 36152536918 (`7813bd1`) and of CI 36232237438 (`a4328974`), both before M7A-DBTEST-ISOLATION-P0; a code-path analysis found that P0 changed nothing that affects it. The mechanism, from matching stack frames and driver source: postgres 3.4.9, with `fetch_types` at its default of true, runs an untagged array-type query on a fresh connection's first ReadyForQuery and discards its promise (`connection.js`, `fetchArrayTypes`). The kernel retires a pool it distrusts with `end({ timeout: 0 })` (`server/persistence/supervisedPgClient.ts`), which a deadline abort such as the limiter's 500 ms bound (`LIMITER_DEADLINE_MS`) reaches. If that destroy lands during the one type-fetch round trip, the type query's rejection has no handler. The production kernel's pools use the default `fetch_types` (`server/platform-identity/db.ts`), and the runtime lifecycle ends the process on any unhandled rejection (`fatal_unhandled_rejection`, `server/runtime/lifecycle.ts`), so a deployed process could exit on the same timing. It is the same class as DA-15 and related to DA-60's load sensitivity. A green rerun is not a repair | M7A-DBTEST-ISOLATION-P0 CI (recorded M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3) | Recorded, not fixed. Owner decision: force `fetch_types: false` in the kernel beside its forced `prepare: false`, after checking that no statement sent through the kernel reads an array column. A test-only fix in the suite would hide the production path. G-IDEMPOT, with DA-15 |

Nothing links it to the created customer's id: `subject_tenant_id` names tenant aggregates only, the writer's metadata allow-list has no customer key, and the command enqueues no outbox event sharing its correlation id. DA-22 had the same gap for a tenant target, closed by 009's column. | DA-112 | **Every tenant instance must hold one `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY`, and nothing checks it.** A record's scope is an HMAC under the process's key. The tenant entry checks only that its own key is present, canonical and different from `RATE_LIMIT_KEY` and its issuer key; whether it equals its peers' cannot be judged by one process. The administrative boundary has the same gap (G-IDEMPOT) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3 | Deployment condition (G-IDEMPOT). A registered key fingerprint, as 014 keeps for `RATE_LIMIT_KEY`, is a later option |
| DA-113 | **READ COMMITTED is a deployment condition for the customer create.** 019's routine takes its locks, then decides on statements whose snapshots are taken after the lock waits, which is sound only under READ COMMITTED. The kernel sets no isolation level, so a database or login whose default transaction isolation is not READ COMMITTED refuses every create (503). Readiness stays green meanwhile: no startup or readiness check reads the setting | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3 | Deployment condition, a G-DBROLE provisioning check. No preflight check was added |

The store proves them equal to the revalidated trusted context. The customer create's mutator proves that context equal to the tenant, actor and grant 019 derived from the session. A holder of the runtime login can still insert a well-shaped tenant-scope audit row directly | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3 | G-AUDIT, with G-DBROLE |
| DA-115 | **019's residuals for a runtime-role holder with a live Create-capable session key.** (1) It chooses the customer id. The 201, id included, is sealed into the completion before COMMIT, so the runtime supplies a version-4 id and the routine accepts any well-formed one. (2) The primary key is global (017), so a 23505 tells it whether an id it chooses exists in any tenant. Random 122-bit ids limit this to confirming ids it already knows. (3) After the pre-check, any live key minted under the installed issuer key — whatever its role or statuses (a technician's, a `read_only` or `overdue` tenant's included) — still takes 016's hold, the session and ledger rows and the issuer-key row before it can be refused. A holder of `tmpos_app` can keep that transaction open indefinitely, not merely until the session expires: the database ends no transaction at session expiry, and a raw session under the role can set its own `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout`. While it stays open it delays that principal's logins and commands, standing and grant changes (the account row FOR UPDATE); 010's status command on, and any update of, every tenant and store that principal's grants name (FOR SHARE); touches, logout and revocation of the held session row (not of the principal's other sessions); every issuer-key rotation; and, once the held ledger row is itself a day past its expiry, 018's prune (run by every tenant login), so every tenant login fails until the holder's transaction ends — the same class as that holder exhausting connections. The account, tenant and store delays it can already cause with 016's hold alone (DA-82). The HTTP path cannot do this: the tenant revalidator refuses a session that is not live before 019 ever runs (`server/composition/tenantAuthorization.ts`, the session read at decision 4), and every command transaction it opens is itself bounded, `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` included (`server/persistence/postgresTransactionalStore.ts`'s `boundsUnder`, applied per transaction by `supervisedPgClient.ts`); only a raw session held outside that path, under the bare runtime role, can hold one open past those bounds. Not a residual: an owner-installed BEFORE INSERT trigger on the customer table that returns NULL cannot make a create commit without its row, because 019 refuses when its INSERT wrote no row (`no_data_found`), so the transaction rolls back and the client gets 503; only the table owner can install such a trigger. *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1: `tmpos_app` no longer executes 019's routine; these residuals carry to 020's `tmpos_role.m7a_customer_create`, now judged on the owner's stored level (DA-118), and 020's role row adds a lock the same holder can keep (DA-120).)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3 | G-DBROLE, with DA-82 and DA-97 |

| DA-122 | **The database does not enforce 020's rollback order.** 020 and the runtime move together: the shared startup preflight requires exactly 001–020 and 020's grants, and the P3 build refuses a 020 database. The documented order must be followed. First, while the 020 runtime still serves, the owner resets every edited role to its default through `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` and the operator confirms the down file's own check finds no non-default level. Then the runtime goes back to the P3 build. Then 020's down runs with its ledger row, which refuses while any stored level differs from its role's default. No runtime serves between the last two steps, since the P3 build refuses a 020 database. No order lets the rollback silently re-widen a narrowed role, because 020's down refuses until every stored level equals its default (the owner's reset widens it deliberately, through the audited route). Running 020's down before the runtime switch, once the levels are reset, also converges: the 020 runtime then fails closed (503) until it is replaced. Switching the runtime to the P3 build before the owner's reset leaves no runtime able to start until the 020 runtime is rolled forward again; the owner then resets the levels through it, and the order above is followed. A PL/pgSQL body records no dependency, so 016's, 017's, 018's or 019's rollback run while 020 is applied succeeds; 020's routines then raise, and 020's down refuses until the chain is whole again. `tests/db/tenantRoleLevelRoutine.integration.test.mjs` M7RL-16 witnesses the down's level guard, its READ COMMITTED refusal, its refusals of a widened grant, the exact restoration of 019's and 018's grants, and a clean re-apply; no test runs the out-of-order 016–019 rollback or starts a runtime across the rollback steps *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4, [ADR-37](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): with 021 applied, 021 is rolled back first. Its down refuses while any version row exists (DA-128) and does not give `tmpos_app` its EXECUTE on 020's set back, so the pre-021 runtime refuses to start until an operator deliberately re-grants it, and that re-grant reopens DA-125. Only then does the order above apply. The database does not enforce this order either: 020's down run while 021 is applied succeeds, dropping the trigger with 020's table; 021's routines then raise, and 021's down refuses until 020 is applied again. `tests/db/tenantRoleLevelRoutine.integration.test.mjs` now runs on 001–020, rolling 021 back first when it is applied and re-applying it at the end)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1 | G-MIGRATE: roll 021 back first (DA-128), then 020 after the owner's reset, each with the code |

| DA-127 | **The shop has no sign-out control in its tenant layout.** A tenant-server logout happens only on a Firebase sign-out (in any tab) or a user switch. So a tenant session can outlive the shop tab until its idle or absolute expiry (30 min idle / 8 h absolute) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2 | Product decision before deployment: a shop sign-out control |
| DA-128 | **021's rollback and re-apply restart the version counter.** 021's down refuses while any version row exists, so no version is discarded by accident. But once the table owner deliberately deletes those rows and rolls 021 back, a re-apply starts every role at version 0 again. A draft still open in a shop tab from before the rollback, at version N, can then match a version N issued after the re-apply against a different level, and its save succeeds (an ABA across the rollback). The down does not re-grant `tmpos_app`'s EXECUTE on 020's edit: while rolled back the runtime role cannot edit a level at all, and the pre-021 runtime refuses to start until an operator re-grants it, which reopens DA-125. `tests/db/roleLevelVersionRoutine.integration.test.mjs` M7RV-12 witnesses the refusal with a version row, the refusal outside READ COMMITTED, the removal once the rows are deleted, no re-grant, and a clean re-apply | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | G-MIGRATE: a rollback of 021 is an owner-accepted risk for every draft still open; no runbook exists to end or invalidate open drafts first |
| DA-129 | **A table owner can change a level without moving its version.** Who can bypass: the owner of 020's `tmpos_role.tenant_role_level`; the owner of 021's schema and `tmpos_role_edit.role_level_version` (whoever applied 021 — 021 accepts an applier who only inherits the owner of 020's table, so the two owners can be different roles); any member of either owner role; and superusers. Four of their writes change a role's effective level and leave the version where it was: an INSERT of a non-NULL level (the insert branch of an upsert and `COPY FROM` included), a DELETE (a return to the default), a TRUNCATE, and disabling or dropping 021's trigger (after which updates move nothing; 021's edit then raises rather than apply). A draft read at that version is then accepted against a level it never saw. The version table's owner can also write or delete version rows directly: runtime and application writes reach that table only through the trigger, but its owner is not so limited. Running 020's `m7a_role_level_set`, which no runtime role can still execute, is not among them: it updates the row, so the trigger advances the version. No runtime role reaches any of this: `tmpos_app` holds no privilege on either table, and 021's postcondition proves it by every path | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | G-DBROLE: hold the owning principal to an owner's standard (as DA-41 and DA-48); owner operations must not write, delete or truncate these tables or disable the trigger outside a migration |
| DA-130 | **020's apply-time postcondition no longer holds once 021 is applied.** 020's postcondition admits no non-internal trigger in schema `tmpos_role` and requires `tmpos_app` to hold EXECUTE on `m7a_role_level_set`. 021 places `trg_tenant_role_level_version_advance` on 020's table and revokes that EXECUTE. 020's file is byte-identical and its check is an apply-time proof, so nothing re-runs it; but a check that re-evaluated 020's postcondition against a 021 database would fail. The standing check is the startup preflight's `APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS`, which reflects 021 | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | Recorded. Any future live role-topology check must use the newest migration's postcondition, not 020's |
| DA-131 | **The customer detail read writes no audit record.** `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}` returns one customer's id and name and appends no `audit_event`, following the list's owner decision (DA-88). Migration 022's routine writes nothing (`tests/db/customerDetail.integration.test.mjs` M7CD-07: no audit, outbox, idempotency, customer, session or level row moves) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | G-AUDIT stays open; decide with DA-88 whether reads of personal data are audited |
| DA-132 | **A direct call of the detail routine.** A holder of the runtime login with `tmpos_app` membership (`tmpos_app` itself is a NOLOGIN role) plus a live minted tenant session key can call `tmpos_customer_detail.m7a_customer_get` directly. It answers only that key's own tenant, under the same standing, level and status rechecks as the route, and a foreign tenant asserted is `denied`. This is the class of 020's `m7a_customer_page_v3` direct call and of DA-119's direct edit, adding no exposure the list's routine does not already give; ids are random, so enumeration is bounded by identifier entropy and session-bound authorization. The per-client rate limiter does not apply here: it applies only to HTTP requests. 022's postcondition checks the runtime ROLES (`tmpos_app`, `tmpos_audit_writer`, `anon`, `authenticated`) through membership, as 020's and 021's do; the runtime LOGIN's shape is enforced by the startup preflight, which M7CD-02b shows refusing an inherited carrier, a SET-only carrier and a direct grant | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | G-DBROLE, with DA-83, DA-93 and DA-119 |
| DA-133 | **The database does not enforce 022's rollback order.** The supported order is 022, then 021, then 020, the ledger's newest-first order. 022's rollback is independent of 021's fail-closed one (M7CD-08). If 020 is rolled back out of order while 022 is applied, 020's down succeeds, because a PL/pgSQL body records no dependency. Then detail reads by `manager`, `technician` and `sales_staff` raise in the routine, which reads 020's dropped table for their level, and the route answers 503: fail closed. `store_owner` reads keep working, because a store owner's Customers access is always Full and the routine never reads 020's table for it (`022_m7a_customer_detail.up.sql:224`, the `store_owner` branch). That is correct authorization, not a leak. 022's own rollback then refuses (its postcondition requires 020's `m7a_customer_page_v3`) until 020 is applied again. `tests/db/customerDetail.integration.test.mjs` M7CD-09 exercises the out-of-order case for a `manager` only; the `store_owner` statement rests on the routine's code, not on that test | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | G-MIGRATE: roll back 022, then 021 (DA-128), then 020 (DA-122), each with its code |
| DA-134 | **Create responses sealed before this build replay without `Location`.** The create's plan before M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2 always sealed `headers: {}`. A same-key replay answers exactly what was sealed, so such a completion replays the 201 without `Location` for the rest of its 24 h retention; the body already carries `customer.id`. Nothing has been deployed, so such records exist only in disposable databases (`server/runtime/customerCreate.test.ts` replays a literal pre-change envelope) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | Recorded; none expected in any deployed environment |
| DA-135 | **Foreign and missing ids are not equalized in time.** The detail routine answers another tenant's id and a missing id with one call, the same SQL statement and the same outcome branch and row. The route answers both with the same status (404), the same error code and body shape and the same bounded log reason. Per-request metadata differs: the body's `requestId`, `X-Request-Id`, `Date` and similar headers. Their timing is not equalized, and the residual is unbounded by design: a foreign id's primary-key probe can find a row that the tenant filter then rejects, and a missing id's cannot. `tests/db/customerDetail.integration.test.mjs` M7CD-05 is a sanity check, not a constant-time proof: it logs the cold and warm medians and asserts they stay within max(2 ms, 25%). Local runs observed medians of about 0.8–1.2 ms for both, as observations only. 022 does not change 017's table, and a tenant-first index would not by itself remove the difference (design review C2.4). Over HTTP, enumeration is limited by the random id space and the per-client rate limiter. A direct routine call (DA-132) is limited only by session-bound authorization and identifier entropy | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | Recorded, not changed |
| DA-136 | **The route surface under `/api/v1/customers/` widened.** Before M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2, `/api/v1/customers/<anything>` was the terminal 404. Now `GET /api/v1/customers/<one raw segment of 1–128 characters without />` matches the detail template. Template lookup is keyed by method, so every other method on such a path is still the terminal 404. It is an authenticated route: the chain answers 401 or 403 first, then a non-canonical segment is 400 `invalid_request`. An empty segment, a trailing slash, an extra segment or a segment of 129 characters or more is still the terminal 404. The router admits this one template by name and refuses every other template, and every literal path directly below its prefix, at registration. *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6, [ADR-41](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the router now admits exactly two templates by name, this one and `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}/profile`, the same segment followed by exactly `/profile`; lookup is an exact literal first, then the template ending in the id, then the template with the id before its suffix. So `GET /api/v1/customers/<segment>/profile` is an authenticated route too: 401 or 403 first, then 400 for a non-canonical segment. Any other extra segment, other method, empty segment or trailing slash is still the terminal 404, and every other template is still refused at registration.)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | Recorded (ADR-38 decision 3); amended by M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 |
| DA-137 | **An unconfirmed customer create is lost on a reload, and after a session change it can no longer be checked from the shop.** The shop's Customer directory page holds an undecided create (its key, its exact body, its name, the session CSRF token it was sent with and a send time) in memory only, and re-sends it only under that same token and within 23 hours. A reload discards the hold. Any new sign-in gives the page another token, and the attempt is then detached for good: after a session expiry (30 min idle / 8 h absolute, DA-127), after the shop is opened in a second tab of the same browser (every page load that opens a server-backed screen signs in, and a login ends the session presented to it: DA-143), or after the account's grant moves. A detached attempt has no "Check again"; its card says it "cannot be checked from here" and that "a row with the same name is not proof". An answer already on its way can still confirm it, shown as `"<name>" was saved on the server under the earlier connection.` (SCD-32, SCD-47); without one, the user is told only that the server may have saved it, and adding the name again uses a new key and can create a second customer. The outcome of a create that settles after its page has gone is kept in the same in-memory store until a page shows it (SCD-46), so it too is lost on a reload. Witnesses: `src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.test.tsx` SCD-26, SCD-27, SCD-32 and SCD-33, and `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs` SCB-06. *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1, [ADR-40](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): a reloaded page, or the shop opened in a second tab, now usually reuses the same session and token (DA-143, closed in code), so the second-tab case above no longer detaches an attempt by itself. An unconfirmed create is still lost on a reload, because the hold is in memory only, but a reload no longer by itself changes the session. Expiry, a moved grant and a different user's sign-in still give the page another token.)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed; amended by M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 ([ADR-40](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)), which leaves this behaviour as it was. Later-stage proposal: a server-provided tenant witness on the session read (the session's `subject` names its user, not its tenant), or a read of an attempt's outcome by its key, so the shop can confirm an attempt across sessions |
| DA-138 | **The customer GET requests are not bound to the session the page just checked.** The directory page reads the session before every request, but the list and detail GETs carry no session token: they are answered under whichever session cookie the browser holds when they arrive. If another tab replaces that cookie between the check and the read (the account signing in again after its grant moved, or another user signing in before this tab sees the change of user), the page can show the other session's rows until its next session check, which comes with its next request or a window focus. It is display only: every create carries the session CSRF token, so the server refuses (403) a create whose cookie session is not the page's, and the page sends no create on a token it has not just re-checked (SCD-31) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed. Later-stage proposal: a server change that binds the reads to the checked session (ADR-39 alternative (c)). **CLOSED IN CODE — M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 ([ADR-40](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** Both customer GETs, cursor pages included, now require `X-TMPOS-Session-CSRF` equal to the presenting session's token and are otherwise refused 403 `forbidden` before the authorizer or any read, and the shop sends the token its session check just answered. Evidence: RB-01 to RB-06 (`tests/db/customerDirectory.integration.test.mjs`) and SCB-08 (`tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs`); commit `5479d1e` (after the dependency commit `df3fc4e`), CI 36791433899 |
| DA-139 | **"Stop tracking" forgets an unconfirmed create.** The button and its warning are offered only while the attempt is shown as not confirmed, not while a first send or a "Check again" is out (SCD-16, SCD-23); a rate-limit wait does not disable it. The warning reads "Stop tracking does not cancel the request, and adding the same name again can create a second customer." It removes the held key and body, so that attempt can never be checked again, and reads the first page; during a rate-limit wait that read is held back until the wait ends (SCD-51). If the server did commit it, the customer is in the directory, and re-adding the name under a new key creates a second one (SCD-34) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed: a deliberate user action with an explicit warning |
| DA-140 | **The add form is offered from the browser's local role state.** The directory page shows its form when the shop's local role state holds Customers at Create or above, and the page itself is reached through the local `customers` feature guard. Neither can see the owner's server-side level (DA-85, DA-124). A user the server does not allow sees the form and gets "Refused by the server. This account may not add customers to this store." with nothing written (SCD-40, SCB-04); a user the server allows but the local state does not is not offered the form, and sees "Your role in this store does not include adding customers." Separately, and unchanged by this stage: the Customers screen's own "+ New Customer" creates a record in the browser's memory only, sends no request and is not checked against the server-side level (`src/components/Customers.localBanner.test.tsx` LCB-02) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed; the server's 403 is the authority. G-IAM, with DA-85 and DA-124 |
| DA-141 | **The shop's tenant clients enforce their response-size cap only after the whole body has been buffered, and do not cancel a non-JSON body.** The customers client (cap 524,288 characters, `src/tenant/customersClient.ts`) and the role-levels client (cap 16,384, `src/tenant/roleLevelsClient.ts`) read a JSON answer in full and compare its length afterwards, so an oversized body is downloaded and held in memory before it is refused (`src/tenant/customersClient.test.ts` CDC-06 witnesses the refusal, not an early stop). The session client (`src/tenant/tenantSessionClient.ts`) parses a 200 JSON answer with no cap of its own. None of the three cancels a body it does not read, such as an answer that is not JSON. What bounds it: a body that is read is cut off by the client's 15-second request timer, and every request is same-origin with redirects refused, so the body comes from the shop's own origin. Reading the body as a stream, to stop at the cap, was considered and not adopted (ADR-39 alternative (f)) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed |
| DA-142 | **A rate-limit wait is forgotten when the connection changes or the page is re-opened.** The directory page disables its read, add and check buttons for the seconds a 429 asked for. While a wait runs, a later deadline wins (a shorter `Retry-After` never shortens it), a wait text states the wait running when it is written (SCD-51), and nothing is read: a customer opened then shows the wait and sends nothing (SCD-52), and a window focus sends no session check (SCD-60). A first-page read the wait blocked (after a confirmed create, a refused create, "Stop tracking" or a cursor reload) is remembered and runs once when the wait ends; it is a read, never a create (SCD-56). But the wait is page state: a new connection resets it and reads again at once, and a page left and re-opened starts without it. Requests can then leave before the server's delay has passed: the first-page read and, on a customer's address, that customer's read. The server's limiter still decides them, so these are wasted requests, not a bypass. Two display limits, with no test: a wait text is not revised when a later answer lengthens the wait, so a customer view can name the shorter wait while its button stays disabled for the longer one; and when two blocked first-page reads fall in one wait, only the later one's note is kept, so "The list changed on the server, so it was loaded again from the start." can be lost | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed |
| DA-143 | **Every page load by a signed-in user that opens a server-backed shop screen signs in to the tenant boundary again, against a login limit of 5 per account per 5 minutes.** The shop's session client remembers a session only in the page's memory. So each page load that opens the role screen's server panel or the Customer directory sends one login, which the two share within a page. The tenant login's account limit is 5 per 5 minutes (G-IDEMPOT row, Limits; `server/runtime/rateLimit.ts`), so a sixth such load inside the window is answered 429. The directory page then shows the wait and no rows; SCD-39 witnesses how the page handles a rate-limited sign-in, not the limit itself. Each login also ends the session the browser presented, which a page already open in another tab sees as a changed connection (DA-137). The role screen has had the same sign-in behaviour since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2; the directory page is its second consumer | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed. Before deployment: decide whether a page load should first read the current session, and whether the account limit suits shop use. **CLOSED IN CODE — M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 ([ADR-40](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** A page load now reads the current session first and reuses it, with no login, when the session 200's new `subject` equals the digest of the signed-in user's UID, so reloads and reopens within the window send no further login; the account limit itself is unchanged and still applies to real sign-ins. Evidence: SCB-07 (one login and no 429 across ten loads in one window) and TSC-15 to TSC-24; commit `5479d1e` (after the dependency commit `df3fc4e`), CI 36791433899 |
| DA-144 | **The Customers screen's records and the server directory are not linked, and nothing is migrated.** The shop now shows customers in two places. The Customers screen holds contact details, loyalty, history and its own create and duplicate rules only in the browser's memory, and they are lost when the page is reloaded. The Customer directory shows the server's id and name. No record is matched by name, no local record gains a server id, and nothing is imported, migrated or erased; a customer added in one place does not appear in the other. Each page says so (the Customers banner, `src/components/Customers.localBanner.test.tsx` LCB-01; the directory's sub-lines, SCD-01; and SCD-43). *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6, [ADR-41](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the directory now also holds an optional phone and email and a creation time per server customer, shown on a customer's page. The Customers screen and its records are unchanged; nothing is imported, no local record gains a server id, and the two are still not linked.)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Product decision (G-PERSIST, G-PRIVACY): the customer record's server fields, their classification and retention, and the cutover from browser memory. G-PERSIST stays OPEN |
| DA-145 | **Locked sidebar items have no programmatic "unavailable" state.** Before activation the shop's sidebar shows the items a store's plan will unlock as plain elements with a lock icon and a hover title, "Complete activation to unlock" (`src/layouts/TenantLayout.tsx`). They carry no role or disabled state that assistive technology can read, so a screen-reader user hears a label with nothing saying it is unavailable. Pre-existing: the Customer Directory entry joins that list under the same rule as every other item (`src/layouts/TenantLayout.customerDirectory.test.tsx` NAV-03) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed. Accessibility review (G-A11Y) |
| DA-146 | **The entry-point suite's bare-import case has a 5 s limit that a cold start on the development host can exceed.** `server/entry/main.test.ts` case 1 imports `main.ts` in a child process with a 5 s limit. On the 8 GB development host it measured 0.5 to 0.6 s warm and 5.1 s cold, killed by the limit. In this stage's final local gate the node lane (`node scripts/run-tests.mjs`, 160 suites) failed on it in two of three runs and passed in the third; alone it passes. The first of those runs, on a loaded host, also failed one case of `server/composition/productionTransactions.test.ts`, which did not recur and whose name was not captured. Neither file is changed by this stage. So a local red on that case is not evidence about a change, and a local green does not show the limit is comfortable; exact-commit CI is the deciding run | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed. Test-reliability review before launch |
| DA-147 | **The role panel's `GET /api/v1/roles/levels` is not bound to the session it checked.** M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 binds only the routes that declare `sessionBinding: 'required'`, the two customer GETs *(three since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6: the profile read declares `sessionBinding: 'required'` too)*. The role screen's server panel reads the levels with no session CSRF token, so the read is answered under whichever session cookie the browser holds when it arrives. If another tab replaced that cookie after the panel's session check, the panel can show that session's levels. The answer names its tenant, and the panel compares it with the tenant its drafts came from (a read naming another tenant clears every draft and says the store changed, [ADR-37](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)). Every PUT carries the session CSRF token and its `expectedTenant`, so nothing is written to the other session's store. It is display only, and only an owner can read the levels | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 | Recorded, not changed. Later-stage proposal: the same binding for the levels read |
| DA-148 | **The tenant session's `subject` is a stable per-user identifier that the session holder can read.** Since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 the tenant session 200s carry `subject`, SHA-256 over a fixed label, the provider and the Firebase UID. It is the same for every session of that user, in every tenant and over time, and it is not keyed: anyone who knows a user's UID can compute it and recognise that user's sessions. Any script on the tenant origin that can call `GET /api/v1/session` with the browser's cookie can read it, as it can already read the session CSRF token. It names no tenant and grants nothing. It is sent only in those two 200 bodies, which every response's `Cache-Control: no-store` covers, and it is never logged | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 | Recorded, not changed. Review with G-PRIVACY: whether a keyed or per-session subject is needed |
| DA-149 | **Session reuse rests on the shop's digest of the Firebase UID, and a reused session is not re-verified with a fresh ID token.** The server does not know which user the page believes is signed in: the shop reuses a session when its `subject` equals the digest it computes from the Firebase UID it holds, and the result is only as current as the page's Firebase state. The server still authorizes every request on the session's own principal, so a wrong comparison can show one identity's data under another's sign-in but grants nothing the cookie does not. A user switch or sign-out still ends the previous session through the shop's auth-state handling ([ADR-36](./10-architecture-decision-records.md) decision 1). Before this stage each page load's login verified a fresh ID token; a reused session now lasts until a logout, its idle or absolute expiry (DA-127) or a server-side revocation, so a change made only at the identity provider (a disabled account, for example) reaches it only through those | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 | Recorded, not changed. Before deployment: decide whether a reused session needs a periodic ID-token re-verification |
| DA-150 | **A reload or a new page whose cookie names an expired session still signs in on its own; only an end seen by an open page waits for Reconnect.** Since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 the shop's session client answers `ended`, with no ID token asked for and no login sent, only for a 401 after it has confirmed this user's session during the page's life. That memory is the page's own and nothing is stored, and the server's 401 does not say whether a session expired, was revoked or never existed. So a reload, a new tab or a page opened later, whose cookie names an ended session, treats the 401 as a first establishment and signs in once through the login and its limits, as before (`src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.test.tsx` SCD-78; TSC-28). A person whose access was removed is still refused by that login (admission), so it re-establishes only what a fresh sign-in would | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 | Recorded, not changed. Later-stage proposal: a server-provided reason on the session read, or a stored marker, if a reload must also wait for Reconnect |
| DA-151 | **Lone surrogates and NUL in a phone or email are refused by the runtime only.** The runtime (`REFUSED_CHARACTER_RE`, `server/runtime/customerDirectory.ts`) and the shop client refuse a C0 control including NUL and a lone surrogate. Migration 023's CHECKs and its create cannot express either: PostgreSQL text cannot hold NUL, and a UTF-8 database cannot hold a lone surrogate, which the driver sends as U+FFFD, a character the CHECKs admit (`tests/db/customerContact.integration.test.mjs` header; `server/runtime/customerCreate.test.ts`). So a direct caller holding `tmpos_app` and a live key (DA-118) can store U+FFFD where the HTTP route would have refused the request. The same split applies to names since 017 | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 | Recorded, not changed |
| DA-152 | **Customers created before migration 023 have no creation time.** 023 adds `created_at` with no default, so every existing row keeps NULL; the default `now()` applies only to rows inserted afterwards, and the migration's time is never written as a creation time. The profile read answers `createdAt: null` and the shop shows "Not recorded" (M7CN-02, M7CN-07; SCD-87). No trustworthy source for those times is recorded: 017 kept no creation time, and the create's audit record names no customer (DA-111), so it cannot be joined to one. Nothing has been deployed, so such rows exist only in disposable databases | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 | Owner decision: any backfill, and from what source. None is proposed |
| DA-153 | **Any role at Customers View or above reads a customer's phone and email.** The profile read is under `view_customers`, the list's and the detail read's permission, so every role whose Customers level is View or above, for example `technician` by default, and any such role under a `read_only` account or tenant (which caps tenant route permissions at View, not below it), can read every customer's phone and email in its tenant. That is the stage's design (the same authority as the detail read), but contact details are personal data, and the read writes no audit record (DA-131's class) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 | Owner decision (privacy, G-PRIVACY, G-IAM): whether contact details need a separate permission, a narrower default, or an audited read |
| DA-154 | **An unresolved create's held body, with its phone and email, stays in that tab's memory until the directory page next renders.** The directory page holds an unconfirmed create's exact body, contacts included, in memory only, so that "Check again" can re-send the same bytes (DA-137). Once the attempt can never be sent again, the body is emptied unless it was name-only: at once on a detach (a connection change), and past the 23-hour check-again cutoff on the page's next render (`src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.test.tsx` SCD-92). An idle page, or one the user has navigated away from, keeps the body in that tab's memory until a render or a reload. It is never displayed (the card shows no contact) and never re-sent | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 | Recorded, not changed |
| DA-155 | **No retention or deletion policy for customer contact data.** Phones and emails are stored in clear on 017's table and kept until a deletion decision exists; there is no delete, edit or export command for them, and 023's rollback refuses while any customer holds a phone, an email or a creation time, so a rollback is never the way to remove them. Bind parameters in a database log expose contacts as they already expose session keys (G-DBROLE). Retention and deletion stay an explicit pre-live decision (owner brief) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 | Owner decision before any live data (G-PRIVACY, G-PERSIST) |
### Authorization findings carried from M5-GAP11-P1-R1 (recorded M5-GAP11-P2; not fixed)

Found by the M5-GAP11-P1-R1 review, outside that stage's scope, and recorded here once. None is fixed, and none blocks the D2 candidate. Each needs an owner decision on whether it must be fixed before the GAP-11 cutover or can be carried to the pre-launch review. *(Retagged M5-GIAM-D1-P3: that cutover was superseded when M5-GAP11-P5 closed GAP-11 at repository level. "Pre-cutover" below now means before the first tenant-plane production route, owner decision D1 under G-IAM.)*

| # | Finding | Where | Disposition |

| AZ-1 | Supervisor refund authorization accepts one hard-coded PIN for every role, and the supervisor it records is a role, not an identified person. As recorded in M5-GAP11-P5, an approval was also kept until the refund completed or was cancelled, was not re-checked when the owner revoked the grant or the POS operator changed, and the POS refund path did not consult the read-only write block. **Resolved in M5-GAP11-P5-R1:** the POS decides a refund again from current state when it executes it (the active operator, that operator's store role, the Refunds level, `approve_refunds`, the plan, the tenant and read-only mode — the write block, or a `read_only` or `suspended` tenant). A supervisor approval covers one refund request and is bound to the signed-in user, the active operator and that operator's role, the tenant and plan, and the supervisor role configuration it was decided on, so an operator change, a revoked grant or an edited Refunds level voids it, and changing it back (re-granting included) does not revive it. Read-only mode denies both the approval and the refund, and the operator's own path needs the explicit `approve_refunds` grant, not only Process Refunds. **Still open:** the shared hard-coded PIN; a per-person supervisor credential; approvals live in memory only, with no durable or audited approval record; every check is client-side, because no server refund command route exists. | `requestSupervisorRefundAuth` and `canExecutePosRefund`, `src/context/AccessContext.tsx`; `decidePosRefundExecution`, `src/context/tenantAccessDecisions.ts`; `src/components/POS.tsx` | Partly resolved (M5-GAP11-P5-R1); open for the PIN, a durable approval record and server enforcement — decision required: pre-cutover or pre-launch |
