### G-DBROLE — the live role-topology check for the transactional store (recorded in M6-PG-P5; not run)

Migration 006 proves only the direct object grants on its own objects, and only when it runs. Before the adapter is bound, the owner-authorized G-DBROLE step must show — read-only, against the real database and the real runtime LOGIN (`<login>` below) — that effective reach still matches those grants. Run every query in a session that first executes `set search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp`, so no operator or function planted in another schema answers in place of pg_catalog's. Each query must return exactly the stated result; anything else stops the binding.

1. **The login.** `select rolsuper, rolinherit, rolcreaterole, rolcreatedb, rolreplication, rolbypassrls, rolcanlogin from pg_catalog.pg_roles where rolname = '<login>'` → `f, t, f, f, f, f, t`.
2. **Its memberships.** `select m.roleid::regrole::text, m.inherit_option, m.admin_option from pg_catalog.pg_auth_members m where m.member = '<login>'::regrole order by 1` → exactly `tmpos_app` and `tmpos_audit_writer`, each `inherit t, admin f`. Then every role it reaches, at any depth: `select r.rolname, r.rolsuper, r.rolcreaterole, r.rolcreatedb, r.rolreplication, r.rolbypassrls from pg_catalog.pg_roles r where pg_catalog.pg_has_role('<login>', r.oid, 'MEMBER') order by 1` → exactly `<login>`, `tmpos_app` and `tmpos_audit_writer`, every attribute `f`. (A store role granted, say, `pg_execute_server_program` — shell commands through `COPY … PROGRAM` — would pass every other step.)
3. **No path to an owner or a superuser.** `select count(*) from pg_catalog.pg_roles r where (r.rolsuper or r.rolbypassrls or r.oid in (select nspowner from pg_catalog.pg_namespace where nspname in ('tmpos_internal', 'public') union select relowner from pg_catalog.pg_class where relnamespace = 'tmpos_internal'::regnamespace or oid = 'public.audit_event'::regclass union select proowner from pg_catalog.pg_proc where pronamespace = 'tmpos_internal'::regnamespace or (pronamespace = 'public'::regnamespace and proname in ('reject_audit_event_mutation', 'audit_metadata_is_flat')))) and pg_catalog.pg_has_role('<login>', r.oid, 'MEMBER')` → `0`. (`MEMBER` counts every path: inherited, `SET`-only and nested. A member of an owner could replace the guard, the clock or the audit functions, or `ALTER` a table.)
4. **Who else reaches the store roles.** `select r.rolname from pg_catalog.pg_roles r where r.oid not in ('tmpos_app'::regrole, 'tmpos_audit_writer'::regrole) and not r.rolsuper and (pg_catalog.pg_has_role(r.oid, 'tmpos_app'::regrole, 'MEMBER') or pg_catalog.pg_has_role(r.oid, 'tmpos_audit_writer'::regrole, 'MEMBER')) order by 1` → only the runtime LOGIN(s) the owner approved, plus at most the principal that created the two roles. For that creator, `select m.roleid::regrole::text, m.admin_option, m.inherit_option, m.set_option from pg_catalog.pg_auth_members m where m.member = '<creator>'::regrole and m.roleid in ('tmpos_app'::regrole, 'tmpos_audit_writer'::regrole) order by 1` → each row `admin t, inherit f, set f`, the `ADMIN`-only membership PostgreSQL 16 gives a non-superuser creator while `createrole_self_grant` is empty (the default). `ADMIN` still lets it grant itself `INHERIT` or `SET` at any time, so the creator is held to an owner's standard: the principal that applies migrations, never an application login, and unreachable from `<login>` (step 2).
5. **Effective reach, for every role.** For each `r` in `pg_catalog.pg_roles` and for `public`, read `has_schema_privilege` on `tmpos_internal` (`USAGE`, `CREATE`) and on `public` (`CREATE`); `has_table_privilege` on each of the two tables (`SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `TRUNCATE`, `REFERENCES`, `TRIGGER`) and `has_column_privilege(r, table, column, p)` for every column and each of `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`; `has_function_privilege` (`EXECUTE`) on every function in `tmpos_internal` — the nine lifecycle routines, `m6_store_clock()` and `outbox_event_transition_guard()`; and `has_table_privilege` on `public.audit_event` (`INSERT`, `SELECT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `TRUNCATE`). → `<login>` holds exactly `USAGE` on `tmpos_internal`, `EXECUTE` on the nine routines and `INSERT` on `public.audit_event`, and nothing else — no table or column privilege and no `EXECUTE` on the clock or the guard (since M6-PG-P6); `tmpos_app` holds the same without that `INSERT`, `tmpos_audit_writer` only that `INSERT`, and any other login step 4 approved no more than `<login>`. The built-in roles `pg_read_all_data` and `pg_write_all_data` report, by design, `USAGE` on `tmpos_internal` and `SELECT` — respectively `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` — on every table and column here, so only superusers may be their members: `select r.rolname from pg_catalog.pg_roles r where not r.rolsuper and r.oid not in ('pg_read_all_data'::regrole, 'pg_write_all_data'::regrole) and (pg_catalog.pg_has_role(r.oid, 'pg_read_all_data'::regrole, 'MEMBER') or pg_catalog.pg_has_role(r.oid, 'pg_write_all_data'::regrole, 'MEMBER'))` → no row. No other role holds any of them, except the owners and superusers (step 3's concern), and no role but an owner or a superuser holds `CREATE` on `public` or `tmpos_internal`.
6. **Section 6, re-run live.** Migration 006's three section-6 selects, read-only (the `with objects … aclexplode …` grants, the functions' owner, definer attribute and pinned path, and the project roles' membership in the owner) → no row each: nothing has been granted, redefined or joined to the owner since the apply.
7. **The guard and row security are in force.** `select tgenabled from pg_catalog.pg_trigger where tgrelid = 'tmpos_internal.outbox_event'::regclass and tgname = 'outbox_event_transition_guard'` → `A`; both tables have `relrowsecurity = t`; `pg_policies` for `tmpos_internal` → no row (since M6-PG-P6 no role but the owner, through the routines, reaches a row).
8. **The login cannot switch the guard off.** `select pg_catalog.has_parameter_privilege('<login>', 'session_replication_role', 'SET')` → `f`. (The trigger is `ENABLE ALWAYS`, so replica mode would not skip it anyway; this checks that the right was not granted.) Step 3 already shows it owns no table it could `ALTER`.

Why steps 2 to 5 read membership and effective reach, not grants alone, is proved against disposable PostgreSQL (M6-PG-20): the `has_*_privilege` functions follow inherited membership (two levels deep) but report `false` for a membership granted `WITH INHERIT FALSE` that `SET ROLE` still reaches; they report `true` for any member of the owner and for a superuser; and a direct table or function grant without USAGE on the schema reaches nothing.

### Runtime database-endpoint classification (recorded M6-PG-P7-R1; persisted M5-ID-P1)

Four clarifications about `classifyRuntimeDatabaseUrl` (`server/platform-identity/databaseEndpoint.ts`), the gate that decides whether an `APP_DATABASE_URL` may be used at all. They record behaviour that already exists; none changes it.

1. **Precedence, in order.** A missing value; a space or control character; an unparseable URL, then a wrong protocol; the host, then the port; a routing (`host`, `hostaddr`, `port`) or pooling (`pgbouncer`, `pool_mode`) query parameter, which is `app_database_endpoint_unsupported`; any *other* `?` or `#`, which is `app_database_url_invalid`; the authority's spelling; the credentials and database name; a pooler username missing its project reference; and last, a username and host naming different projects. So an endpoint refused as unsupported is never also reported as invalid, and the pooling refusal is reached before the generic query-parameter one.
2. **Identical to the migration executor's managed-DSN validator:** the postgres scheme; the direct and pooler host patterns, and the refusal of any other `db.` host; port 5432 only; the `pgbouncer` and `pool_mode` rules; the role pattern; the refusal of a username/host project mismatch; and a required password.
3. **Stricter at runtime than the executor:** the value is judged as written, so any whitespace or control character is refused; `hostaddr` and `port` are refused as well as `host`; *every* occurrence of a pooling parameter is checked, not just the first; any other query parameter or fragment is refused outright; the authority must be spelled exactly (`:05432`, an empty `:`, or two `@` are refused); the database name must have a valid shape; and a NUL in the password is refused. The executor alone additionally checks agreement with the project URL and the expected database name — a managed-target concern the runtime has no equivalent of.
4. **Composition is route-ready; the deployable entry is routeless (until M6-ENTRY-P1).** `server/composition/productionTransactions.ts` classifies the endpoint before anything is built, but its production inventory holds no route, so nothing is read, classified or connected today; the deployable entry (`server/runtime/server.ts`) serves the probes and the bounded fallback only. *Since M6-ENTRY-P1, `server/runtime/server.ts` is deleted and the deployable entry (`server/entry/productionEntry.ts`) serves exactly the approved inventory — the two probes and the six administrative routes, then the bounded 404 — and the transaction root classifies `APP_DATABASE_URL` for those routes before anything is built; in code only, not deployed.* Separately, the unclassified `getRuntimeDb()` client — which skips this classifier — has exactly three callers, all DEV-only and none reachable from a deployable entry: `db.ts` (defines it), `authorizationRepository.ts` (the DEV control plane) and `platform-identity/server.ts` (the DEV sidecar's readiness probe, which binds a listener only under `npm run identity:api`). The audit writer's former fallback to it was **removed in M5-ID-P1**, and `tests/quality/db-client-containment.test.mjs` now pins that caller set. *Since M6-ENTRY-P1, `db.ts` is in the entry's graph (the entry imports it), so of those three it alone is now reachable from a deployable entry, and its two DEV client factories, `getDb()` and `getRuntimeDb()`, ship uncalled (DA-58).*

### The distributed rate-limit store — operating limits (recorded M6-RATE-P2; [ADR-26](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))

What migration 014 and `server/persistence/postgresRateLimiter.ts` do in operation, and what a deployment must still provide. None of it is live: no deployable entry composes the store *(until M6-ENTRY-P1; since then the deployable entry, `server/entry/productionEntry.ts`, composes it through the transaction root in code only, not deployed)*.

1. **Failure.** Any outage, timeout (lock 200 ms, statement 300 ms, idle-in-transaction 400 ms, and the 500 ms limiter cap), abort, malformed answer or key mismatch refuses the request with a bounded 503, logged as `rate_limit_unavailable` or `rate_limit_timeout`, and never allows it. The adapter answers at its 500 ms bound even with COMMIT in flight; an allowance committed after it is answered unavailable, so its cost is spent and no allowance goes unrecorded. The same conditions make the probe false, and so does a read-only session or a standby (`store_read_only`), so `/readiness` answers 503; `/health` never touches the limiter. There is no local fallback while PostgreSQL is down.
2. **Load.** Every protected request is at least one short transaction on the primary database, which writes whenever the consume is allowed; a login is up to three. A consume the committed state already refuses (a live window whose count plus the cost exceeds the limit) is answered from an unlocked read: no row lock and, once the key fingerprint is registered, no transaction ID and no WAL; a refusal racing the consume that spends the last allowance can still take the locked path. These transactions share the runtime kernel's ten one-connection pools (`RUNTIME_POOL_MAX` in server/platform-identity/db.ts, at the supervised client's `MAX_POOLS` ceiling) with sessions, admission and commands. The adapter serializes consumes per bucket per instance, so one source holds at most one connection per bucket it reaches, however many requests it sends — a source that reaches several buckets can still hold several connections, and many sources can exhaust the pool (DA-52); at most 64 consumes of one bucket wait per instance, the one in flight included (`RATE_LIMIT_LANE_DEPTH`), and past that a consume of that bucket is a 503 at once. A consume aborted before its COMMIT leaves its transaction open on the server until the server sees the retired session end, so the next consume of that bucket can wait on the row and is a 503 past its 200 ms lock bound (DA-52). Many sources can still exhaust the ten connections, which the edge must absorb (DA-52).
3. **Cleanup.** Never inside a consume. After an allowed consume that opened a window, the adapter calls `m6_rate_limit_reclaim()` in the background, in its own transaction under 50 ms lock, statement and idle bounds. Each such consume the adapter answers owes one call (one whose window opened only after its caller was answered unavailable owes none); owed calls run one at a time per instance, the next only after one deleted a full four, and one that deletes fewer, or fails, clears what is owed. Each call deletes at most four expired buckets, oldest first, through the expiry index, skipping locked rows, each by its primary key; its failure changes no consume. Its lock hold is bounded far below a consume's 200 ms lock wait, so a consume of an expired bucket being reclaimed still succeeds. The probe never cleans, and there is no timer or worker. So after a burst each window opened is matched by up to four reclaimed while a backlog remains and reclaims succeed; during a burst of new clients windows can open faster than one reclaim at a time clears them, and with no traffic the backlog stays. Under a long-held database snapshot (a long transaction, a replication slot, `pg_dump`), vacuum cannot clear the dead entries that window reopens (non-HOT updates) and reclaims leave at the low end of the expiry index; with about 100,000 of them (a one-off check on a throwaway local PostgreSQL 16 cluster, not part of the committed tests) both reclaims tried failed at the 50 ms statement bound (SQLSTATE 57014) until the snapshot ended. Storage grows meanwhile; consumes, which use the primary key, are unaffected. Monitor reclaim failures with SQLSTATE 57014, long transactions and the xmin horizon's age, and the table's bloat (DA-52).
4. **The key.** `RATE_LIMIT_KEY` must be identical on every instance. The first consume or probe registers its fingerprint in the database; an instance with any other key answers every protected request 503 and fails readiness. If a wrongly keyed instance calls first after 014 is applied, every correctly keyed instance is locked out that way. A holder of the runtime database credential can do the same on purpose without the key, by calling the consume or the probe first with a fingerprint of its choosing between 014's apply (or a re-apply after rollback) and the first correctly keyed call; that locks every instance out, fails closed and grants no allowance (DA-53). Runbook step: right after applying 014, have a correctly keyed instance call (its readiness probe registers the key) before anything else holds the `tmpos_app` credential, or have the owner confirm that the registered fingerprint equals HMAC-SHA256 keyed with the bytes `RATE_LIMIT_KEY` decodes to (not its text) over the ASCII string `tmpos-rate-limit-key-fingerprint:v1`, as `rateLimitKeyFingerprint` computes it. Recovery is an operator procedure: stop the wrongly keyed instances, confirm the intended key's fingerprint, delete the one `key_fingerprint` row as the owner in a maintenance window, and let a correctly keyed instance register. No routine does it, and nothing does it automatically. Rotation needs a coordinated procedure that does not exist (DA-53). 014's rollback also removes the fingerprint, drops every bucket with it, refuses first unless its transaction is READ COMMITTED (at a stricter level an earlier statement's snapshot could hide a window opened while its lock was awaited), takes its locks before its check, and refuses while any window is live.
5. **Trusted proxies (deployment work, unchanged by this stage).** `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS` must list exactly the real edge's egress ranges, covering every hop and nothing wider. The edge must append to or overwrite `X-Forwarded-For` and be the runtime's only way in, and must present IPv4 clients as IPv4 or IPv4-mapped addresses (see G-IDEMPOT on NAT64). No real edge or proxy has been exercised, so that contract is unproved (G-UNAUTH, DA-9).
6. **Measured local limits.** A bounded concurrency check on a local disposable PostgreSQL 16.10 cluster over a Unix socket, run twice: with durable commits (`fsync` and `synchronous_commit` on, as CI's stock server) and with `fsync` off. It is not a production throughput claim: it says nothing about a managed database, network latency, pool contention with real sessions and commands, or failover.
   - Concurrency: 2 limiter instances on 2 kernels of 6 connections each, so 12 transactions in flight (32 concurrent callers).
   - Consumes: 2,000 — 600 on one hot bucket (limit 500) and 1,400 over 400 cold keys (limit 3).
   - Duration: durable commits 2,536 ms (789 consumes/s), latency p50 6.96 / p95 159.48 / p99 171.64 / max 193.59 ms; `fsync` off 1,352 ms (1,480/s), p50 4.81 / p95 75.82 / p99 89.45 / max 101.34 ms.
   - Errors and timeouts: in both runs 1,340 allowed, 660 limited, 0 unavailable, 0 database errors, 0 faults; background reclaims 162 (durable) and 95 started, at most 1 in flight per instance, 0 failed.
   - Exact accounting: held in both runs — 0 buckets over-allowed, 0 under-counted. One bucket, 120 concurrent consumes over 4 instances with limit 40 under durable commits: exactly 40 allowed, 80 limited, 0 unavailable, in 211–277 ms across runs.
   - One hot bucket's ceiling: an earlier durable run of 200 concurrent consumes of one bucket (limit 100, before refusals became lock-free) lost 76 to the 500 ms deadline. With durable commits one bucket grants on the order of a hundred allowances per 500 ms, since each allowance holds the row through its WAL flush.

## Summary

- **Total gates:** 37. **BLOCKER:** 21 · **HIGH:** 14 · **MED:** 2. — unchanged since M1. **Lockfile portability is a mandatory subcriterion of G-APPSEC, not a new gate:** G-APPSEC already owns the dependency (SCA) audit, and that audit reads `package-lock.json`, so lock fidelity is a precondition of G-APPSEC's own pass rule rather than a separate risk. No existing gate had claimed lock integrity, clean installation, or supply-chain reproducibility, and none needed to be stretched to accept it. The pre-install checker is enforced regardless of this accounting; only the governance ownership is consolidated. Its non-waivability is a **rule**, not an assertion — see the non-waivable carve-out below.
- **Current state:** all `OPEN` at M1 (M1 is planning; it opens the register, it does not close gates). M2 moves G-EMU, G-APPSEC and G-COVERAGE to `PARTIAL`. **Phase 4.0 M3 (Shipping sidecar elimination) additionally moves G-SSRF and G-SECRETS to `PARTIAL`** — each had a distinct M3 half (eliminate the request-controlled fetch; remove the in-memory unauthenticated credential store) that is now done with regression evidence, while the remainder (M7e/M8 egress boundary; M6/M8 encrypted rotatable secrets) stays open. **Phase 4.0 M3 (shared enforced runtime middleware) moves G-UNAUTH to `PARTIAL`**: the Shipping and identity routes are eliminated and the M3 runtime admits requests only through one shared enforced chain. **The M3 body/media-type policy and the M4 login/session foundation (both committed in `dd89ff9`) keep G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL` and leave G-CPLOGIN `OPEN`:** the body policy is delivered, and the runtime now carries both session boundaries, session-bound CSRF, login limits and the Firebase adapter adapted to the authenticator's port shape (composed only by the provider-aware composition root, which refuses) — but the deployable artifact composes no authenticator, admission source or session store, so production wiring (a deployable build of the composition root, a durable session store, a production admin admission adapter — G-DBROLE — and tenant/store admission — migration 005 + M5), a trusted-proxy contract and HSTS, distributed rate limits (M6), step-up and admin-hostname provisioning, the legacy DEV identity/BCP app and the deployed-endpoint pass rule (M5/M8) stay open. **M4 session-lifecycle hardening (committed in `dd89ff9`) keeps G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL` and G-CPLOGIN `OPEN`:** the provider-aware composition root, bounded port calls, admission revalidation, per-principal revocation, admin MFA and recent authentication, and host-distinct origins are delivered in code, but production login is not active — the composition root refuses until a durable session store and the admission and authorizer adapters exist (G-DBROLE; migration 005 unexecuted; M5). **M4-ADMIN-UI-P1 (committed in `dd89ff9`) keeps G-CPLOGIN `OPEN` and G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL`:** the admin sign-in and read-only control-panel shell are delivered in the browser, but production login is not active and no endpoint was added; the concurrent admission-revalidation requirement (G-CPLOGIN row) now blocks every sensitive admin action and production session activation. **M4-ADMIN-UI-P2 (committed in `dd89ff9`) moves G-WEBHARDEN to `PARTIAL` (admin half):** the admin header policy, the listener and the routing boundary exist in code, but no admin-host deployment exists and the tenant SPA is M8. It keeps G-CPLOGIN `OPEN` and G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL` — it adds no unauthenticated route, and the read-only Command Center route is session-authenticated, authorized and composed nowhere — and leaves G-DBROLE and G-MIGRATE `OPEN`, with migration 005 unexecuted and not modified by this stage. **M6-RATE-P1 (committed in `dd89ff9`) keeps G-IDEMPOT `OPEN` and G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL`:** the trusted-proxy contract and the distributed limiter port, its conformance suite and the runtime integration exist in code, but no shared store is approved or composed, the deployment half of the proxy contract is not done, and durable idempotency is not started. **M6-IDEMPOT-P2 (`a6469f23`) and M6-OUTBOX-P3 keep G-IDEMPOT and G-AUDIT `OPEN`:** the idempotency, command-transaction and outbox contracts exist in code with their conformance suites, but no adapter, schema migration or worker exists and nothing is composed. **M6-PG-P4 keeps G-IDEMPOT, G-AUDIT, G-MIGRATE and G-DBROLE `OPEN`:** the PostgreSQL adapter and migration 006 exist and pass the three conformance suites against disposable PostgreSQL, but nothing is composed, 006 is applied to no managed database, and the managed apply of 005 refuses while 006 is pending. **M6-PG-P5 and M6-PG-P6 keep them `OPEN`:** they harden the adapter and close DA-16 through DA-20 — the runtime role reaches the store only through 006's fixed lifecycle routines, and every store transaction runs through the application's own transaction kernel — but nothing is composed, 006 is applied to no managed database, and no runtime LOGIN exists. M4's production blockers (the G-CPLOGIN admission race, G-DBROLE, migration 005) are separate and unchanged. **M5-ID-P1 keeps G-DBROLE, G-CPLOGIN, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT and G-MIGRATE `OPEN`, and advances a named half of three of them.** Migration 007 creates its own schema, `tmpos_identity`, holding three routines (corrected in M5-GAP11-P1-R1: an earlier revision said 006's `tmpos_internal`) — no table, no column, no role, no second credential — through which the runtime resolves a **provider-verified** reference to the app-owned `internal_user_id`, its status, a security version and its usable membership grants, and re-reads that authority inside a committing transaction. **G-DBROLE:** the identity-read half is closed in code — `tmpos_app` holds no privilege on `platform_identity`, `app_user` or `identity_link` and reaches them only through those `SECURITY DEFINER` routines, so the path a production route will take never needs the OWNER connection that `authorizationRepository.ts` uses today; the credential half (a runtime LOGIN role and its endpoint) is untouched and still blocks. Note precisely what did NOT happen: **no existing caller was converted.** `authorizationRepository.ts` still reads those relations through the OWNER connection, and it is untouched by this stage — it is the DEV control plane, reachable from no deployable entry, and replacing it is later M5 work. What 007 establishes is that the path a production route WILL take never needs that connection, and a containment test now refuses a direct identity read from `server/runtime`, `server/persistence` or `server/composition`. **G-CPLOGIN:** the pre-operation requirement of this register (a sensitive handler re-verifying the principal's security version inside its own write transaction) is delivered for the command path — after the idempotency fence, before any business state, every row read `FOR SHARE` in one fixed order so a racing revocation is deterministic, and a failure commits no mutation, completion, audit record or event; the admission-revalidation race, step-up, the admin hostname and production login are untouched and still block. **G-AUDIT:** actor attribution is delivered for a command carrying a trusted context — its AUDIT row names the app-owned actor, never a caller-supplied value — while full coverage remains M6/M5. An outbox event still names no actor, tenant or store: migration 006 types those columns as keyed digests rather than identifiers, and this stage keys none. Nothing is composed into a deployable entry, migration 007 is applied to **no** managed database (disposable PostgreSQL only, as 005 and 006 are), **no business route exists and none is live**, and tenant- and store-scope permissions stay undecidable until GAP-11's ordering unification and its six mandatory safeguards land ([04 §3](./04-canonical-iam-and-four-user-migration.md)). **Correction (M5-GAP11-P1):** an earlier revision of this bullet said two of those safeguards "are the owner's". Read against the source, only **safeguard #3** is: 04 §3 says the diff "is reviewed and explicitly owner-approved before cutover". **Safeguard #1**, the per-action re-pin, is an *open policy choice* — 04 §3 offers two ways to satisfy it ("require `≥ manage` **or** an explicit per-role grant") and picks neither; it never assigns the choice to the owner. Calling it an owner decision overstated the source document. It is tracked here as decision **D2** because someone must choose, not because 04 §3 says the owner must. **M5-GAP11-P1 keeps G-IAMUNIFY and G-IAM `OPEN`, and delivers the evidence-and-observation half of GAP-11.** It commits the generated before/after effective-grant diff ([evidence/gap11-ordering-flip-grant-diff.md](./evidence/gap11-ordering-flip-grant-diff.md)): 13 of 1,659 canonical tuples change (12 widened, 1 narrowed), every one a tenant domain-threshold row; no named sub-permission and no platform row moves. It adds a dual-read shadow comparator that returns the caller's decision unchanged and is wired into no decision path, and an exhaustive authorization-matrix suite. G-IAMUNIFY's own pass rule ("One ordering; no effective grant silently changed; tests prove parity") is untouched: the tenant engine still ranks `manage < approve`, nothing was re-pinned (safeguard #1, decision D2) and nothing is approved (safeguard #3, decision D3). The diff is the evidence that pass rule needs; it is not the unification. One safeguard-#4 gap is recorded rather than fixed: the shipped comparators (`rankIn` in `permissionCatalog.ts`, `levelIndex` in `permissionDecision.ts`) rank an unknown *required* level as `none`, so every holder clears it. That is unreachable today — no catalog-defined requirement is unknown and route requirements are typed constants — but the cutover must close it. **M5-GAP11-P1-R1 keeps G-IAMUNIFY and G-IAM `OPEN` and closes that safeguard-#4 gap:** every shipped authorization entry point — the catalog's comparators and materializers, the DEV decision spine (`permissionDecision.ts`, `protectedAction.ts`), the durable-state resolver, the M5 route catalog, the BCP pilot guard, and the client engines — now denies an unknown or malformed role, plane, feature, domain, action, required or held level, explicit grant value, status or ambiguous configured authority, each with direct CI-run evidence, and every valid canonical decision is unchanged. It also corrects P1's taxonomy: `approveGated` only ever meant that a tuple's decisive level is `approve`; it is now `requiresApproveLevel`, and a separate D2 classification marks a tuple a money action only where an authoritative document says so — 17 tuples, none of which the unified ordering changes. The twelve widened rows are `approve`-level but `unresolved`, not D2 rows. D1–D4 remain undecided, and GAP-11 stays open at least until safeguards #1 (D2) and #3 (D3) land — the ordering cutover itself, #5's switch of authority and #6's intended-outcome assertions are also still required. **M5-GAP11-P2 and M5-GAP11-P3 keep G-IAMUNIFY and G-IAM `OPEN`:** owner decisions D2 (explicit per-role money-action grants) and D3 (reject all thirteen ordering changes, and conditionally approve the resulting zero-diff artifact) are carried in the non-authoritative candidate only. That candidate now answers exactly as the shipped authority on all 1,659 tuples, but the tenant engine still ranks `manage < approve` and no authority has been switched, so the pass rule stays untouched until a separately authorized cutover; D1 and D4 remain undecided. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED) — no gate was added, removed, downgraded, or closed.** **No gate is `PASS`.** **M5-GAP11-P5 closes GAP-11 and G-IAMUNIFY at repository level ([ADR-19](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** the global-ordering cutover is superseded; the store and platform families keep their own orderings behind one family contract, every comparison names its family, and money approval is an explicit editable grant. The D2 table becomes the built-in money-grant defaults, the thirteen D3 pins are retired from active evaluation, and the P2/P3 records stay as history. G-IAMUNIFY is now the only `PASS`, at repository level (exact-commit CI is recorded in the stage report); the gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED). G-IAM stays `OPEN`, D1 and D4 remain undecided, durable role-editor persistence is still open, and AZ-1 to AZ-8 are not remediated. **M5-GAP11-P5-R1 completes the money-action enforcement G-IAMUNIFY's `PASS` rests on:** the POS decides a refund again when it executes it, binds a supervisor approval to one request and to what it was decided on, and refuses both in read-only mode; every existing billing refund and credit entry point consumes `approve_billing_actions` when offered and again in its handler. That resolves the stale-approval and read-only part of AZ-1 and the `approve_billing_actions` part of AZ-6. The rest of AZ-1 (the shared PIN, a durable approval record), the other 16 sub-permissions of AZ-6, and AZ-2 to AZ-5, AZ-7 and AZ-8 stay open. These money controls are client-side only until a server command route exists; durable role and approval storage stay open; G-IAM stays `OPEN`. **M5-GIAM-D1-P1 records owner decision D1 and implements the first server-authorized business command ([ADR-20](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)): a tenant's display name.** `PATCH /admin/v1/tenants/display-name` — the brief's `/api/platform/tenants/:tenantId/display-name` in the form the route conventions call for (platform routes live under the admin boundary's `/admin/v1`, and paths are literal, so the tenant travels in the body) — runs the one enforced chain: framing, the client limit, CSRF and origin, the admin session, then the canonical platform authorizer, which resolves the session's principal to its app-owned actor through migration 007, selects that actor's one platform grant and decides `edit_tenant_profile` (Tenants at Edit or above) from the role's definition, then the bounded body, then the UUID-v4 idempotency key, then the body's closed validation (a refused body is a 400 that records nothing), then one transaction: the lease fence, the actor's revalidation, migration 008's fixed operation (a row lock, the version before the name), the audit record and one `tenant.display_name_changed` v1 event — or none of them. A name already in place is a 200 that writes nothing but the idempotency completion. **G-IAM stays `OPEN`** — one platform command is its first server-enforced slice, not canonical IAM. It advances named halves of three gates: **G-IDEMPOT** (the first business route over the durable store and transaction port, proven against disposable PostgreSQL), **G-AUDIT** (a real business mutation audited with its resolved actor and linked by correlation to its event) and **G-CPLOGIN** (the command-path revalidation now guards a real write). Each stays `OPEN`, and so do G-DBROLE (no runtime LOGIN or endpoint), G-MIGRATE (008 and 009 disposable-only; D4 recorded above, now through 009) and G-UNAUTH. Nothing is composed into a deployable entry, the admin boundary's production session store and admission still do not exist, **no route is live**, and no client binds the command yet: no display-name editor exists, so the UI binding is the next product stage. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M5-GIAM-D1-P2 adds the second server-authorized command — tenant suspension and reactivation — and closes DA-31 ([ADR-22](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** `PATCH /admin/v1/tenants/status` moves a tenant between `active` and `suspended` and nothing else: a target equal to the current status is an invalid transition (409), never a no-op, and the other four values of 002's status domain are neither reachable nor a state a transition may start from. It requires `change_tenant_status` (tenant_management at Manage or above, sensitive — a permission the canonical catalog already defined and no route had used), a bounded 10-500 scalar operator reason recorded on the durable audit row through its existing reason pair, and a TRUSTED RECENT AUTHENTICATION: the provider's own verified authentication time, bound to the session at login, no older than ten minutes, refused as a bounded `step_up_required` and re-judged inside the committing transaction against the DATABASE's clock. Migration 010 adds `tenant.access_version` — the witness a tenant/store session must be admitted against, moved by exactly one per transition and never back, and READ BY NOTHING yet, since the admission adapter that must report it does not exist (DA-32) — and one fixed SECURITY DEFINER operation; **nothing is revoked, because nothing ever reached those columns**: no role these migrations own holds a privilege on `tenant.status`, and the new column is granted to nobody, which 010's verify proves rather than asserts (direct, inherited and SET-only paths all tested). The runtime gains ONE tenant-state decision (GAP-13) — active continue, read-only deny writes, suspended deny, anything unrecognised deny — which scope selection now applies, so a suspended or unknown-status tenant selects no scope at all; and the tenant boundary re-asks admission on every protected request, so once an admission adapter exists a suspension will have no grace period. **G-IAM, G-AUDIT, G-CPLOGIN and GAP-13 all stay `OPEN`:** the tenant/store session boundary is composed nowhere (DA-32), so every session effect is proved in code and against disposable PostgreSQL and exercised by no live session; migration 010 is applied to no managed database (D4 now names the contiguous chain through 010); no route is live; and no client binds either command (DA-34 — the UI binding is the immediate next product stage). **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **Phase 4.0 M3 S2/S2.2 (database principal separation) leaves G-DBROLE `OPEN`, deliberately:** it added the NOLOGIN privilege roles, the tenant/store RLS policies, the least-privilege grant matrix, migration-owned role lifecycle, and a fail-closed effective-privilege gate — but it converted **no** caller, provisioned **no** LOGIN role, and applied to **no** managed database, so the gate's own pass rule (tenant paths actually running under RLS as a non-owner role) is untouched by it. **M5-GIAM-D1-P2-R1 keeps G-DBROLE, G-CPLOGIN, G-IAM, G-AUDIT, G-MIGRATE and GAP-13 `OPEN` and closes DA-32.** The tenant boundary now HAS the two adapters it never had — a durable PostgreSQL session store and a PostgreSQL admission that derives, from the actor's whole standing including each tenant's access version, the one opaque witness the boundary binds a session to and re-reads on every protected request — and the production transaction root builds both over the client it already owns. Against disposable PostgreSQL and real local sockets, a suspension refuses the very next request and revokes the session, a login while suspended is refused, and after reactivation the witness differs from the pre-suspension one while the principal's own security version is identical. Three things are stated rather than claimed away: **no production tenant business route exists** (every tenant-scope permission is undecidable until owner decision D1, and the transaction root refuses to start with one), so the proof runs against a witness route defined only in the integration harness and refused in production source and in the artifact by containment tests (DA-39); both session boundaries share one table and one runtime role (DA-37); and a protected request costs two admission reads (DA-38). No route is live, 011 is applied to no managed database, and the admin boundary's own ADMISSION adapter still does not exist — it is bound to the same durable store as the tenant boundary, which is DA-37, not a store of its own. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M5-GIAM-D1-P3 keeps G-IAM, G-CPLOGIN, G-DBROLE, G-AUDIT and G-MIGRATE `OPEN`, and binds the two platform commands to a client ([ADR-24](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It adds one platform read, `GET /admin/v1/tenants`, under `view_tenants` with a required trusted context. The request carries no body and no idempotency key, and its query is strict: any other key, a repeat or an encoding is a 400 before any read. The route reads through migration 012's one `STABLE SECURITY DEFINER` routine in its own schema `tmpos_directory`, which confirms the named platform grant in the same snapshot as a keyset page ordered by `(created_at, tenant_id)`. The admin console's Tenant Management workspace now lists, shows, renames, suspends and reactivates tenants through the server alone, with no optimistic state and one idempotency key per intent (DA-34). **Recorded deviation:** the stage brief said a schema migration was not expected. 012 exists because `tmpos_app` sees `public.tenant` only under migration 005's tenant/store-scoped row policy, so no platform listing can be a query the runtime runs; the owner was asked and replied "continue". 012 creates no table, no role and no write, is applied to no managed database (disposable PostgreSQL only), and D4 now names the contiguous chain through 012; 005–011 are byte-identical. What stays open is stated rather than claimed away. The tenant plane still has no production route (G-IAM/D1). No step-up flow exists: a `step_up_required` refusal asks the person to sign in again. The store lifecycle stays unexposed because of DA-41, and DA-42 to DA-45 are recorded. Nothing is deployed or composed into a deployable entry, and no route is live. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M5-GIAM-D1-P4 keeps G-IAM, G-CPLOGIN, G-DBROLE and G-MIGRATE `OPEN` and G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL`, and makes the administrative boundary's admission and authorization durable and production-composed in code ([ADR-25](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** Migration 013 adds schema `tmpos_admission`: a per-actor platform authority version that only increases and has no foreign key, moved by four `ENABLE ALWAYS` row triggers on one definer function — on `platform_identity` (anchor columns only), `app_user` and `identity_link` (any column but `updated_at`) and `user_membership` (platform scope only, any column but `updated_at`) — and one `STABLE SECURITY DEFINER` admission read, which answers 007's resolution narrowed to platform grants, plus that version, in one snapshot. Its rollback and a re-application each refuse while any administrative session exists, because a login racing the rollback can commit its session after it and the counters restart at zero. It exists because 007's version excludes memberships, its timestamp digest can be reproduced (an exported row re-inserted, a replica-mode write) and does not cover the identity anchor, and a witness built from the grant row returns when the grant is disabled and re-enabled. The transaction root builds the PostgreSQL platform admission over its one kernel, and the sessions root binds the admission and the canonical authorizer together. Handed that bundle, neither `admin_admission_unavailable` nor `admin_authorizer_unavailable` arises. `rate_limit_store_unavailable` remains, so production composition still refuses *(until M6-RATE-P2, below)*, no production entry composes either root, and nothing is deployable. Administrative sessions now re-admit on every protected request (it was a 1-minute window) against a `p1:` witness; a denial revokes the principal's administrative sessions only, a witness change revokes the session presented, an outage revokes nothing, and a restoration of the standing the witness covers revives nothing (a code-defined role definition is outside it: DA-47). Readiness includes the administrative admission's probe; the tenant admission's is still not wired. 013 is applied to no managed database (disposable PostgreSQL only), 005–012 are byte-identical, and D4 now names the contiguous chain through 013. GAP-11 stays closed at repository level. DA-34 and DA-41 to DA-45 are carried unchanged; DA-46 to DA-51 are recorded. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M6-RATE-P2 keeps G-CPLOGIN, G-IDEMPOT, G-IAM, G-DBROLE and G-MIGRATE `OPEN` and G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL`, and adds the distributed rate-limit store ([ADR-26](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** Migration 014 adds schema `tmpos_rate_limit`: shared fixed-window buckets keyed by pseudonymous limiter keys, the registered fingerprint of `RATE_LIMIT_KEY`, and consume, probe and reclaim routines, the only routines `tmpos_app` may execute there; it holds no table privilege, and no other runtime-facing role may execute any of them. The PostgreSQL adapter implements the unchanged port, and the unchanged conformance suite passes against it on disposable PostgreSQL. The transaction root builds it over its one kernel, and the sessions root binds it with its own parsed key, so a composition handed that bundle and a valid key no longer names `rate_limit_store_unavailable`. That narrows G-CPLOGIN's rate-limit part and G-IDEMPOT's limiter half, and closes neither: no production entry composes either root (the deployable entry is the next composition task), and nothing is deployable. It fails closed — a store outage or a mismatched key is a 503 and a failing readiness probe, never an allowance — and it adds write load to the primary database (DA-52). `RATE_LIMIT_KEY` can be neither rotated nor, after a wrongly keyed instance registers first, re-registered without an operator procedure (DA-53). The real edge and proxy contract is unproved (G-UNAUTH, DA-9). 014 is applied to no managed database (disposable PostgreSQL only), 001–013 are unchanged, and D4 now names the contiguous chain through 014. DA-46 to DA-51 are carried unchanged, with DA-47 restated explicitly; DA-9 is updated; DA-52 and DA-53 are recorded. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M6-ENTRY-P1 keeps G-CPLOGIN, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-IAM, G-AUDIT and G-IDEMPOT `OPEN` and G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL`, and adds the deployable production entry in code ([02 §2.1](./02-target-production-architecture.md), [ADR-27](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** The entry (`server/entry/productionEntry.ts`) composes the sessions and transaction roots with the administrative boundary alone — no entry composes the tenant boundary — and serves exactly the 8 shipping routes, then the bounded 404; every route but the two probes answers only on the admin host, whose Host the runtime now checks before the limiter (the real edge must route that hostname alone and pass Host through unaltered: DA-9, unproved). It listens only after 10 bounded startup steps, among them a read-only preflight of the runtime login's standing and of the migration ledger (exactly 001–014; nothing is applied, rolled back or repaired). That narrows G-CPLOGIN's deployable-entry part and G-DBROLE's enforced-startup-preflight part, and closes neither: no admin-host deployment exists, the step-up experience (DA-34) and the login/logout audit record are not delivered, and the pre-operation security requirement is not re-judged; the persistent runtime LOGIN and its endpoint, its new direct SELECT on `public.schema_migrations` (which no migration grants) and the owner-side database-ACL provisioning are operator actions not taken. G-AUDIT and G-IDEMPOT gain only the entry's in-code composition of the existing adapters for the two audited administrative mutations. No migration is added (001–014 are unchanged, and D4 still names the chain through 014), nothing is deployed, and only disposable PostgreSQL was contacted. DA-46 to DA-53 are carried unchanged; DA-9 gains a dated clause for the admin Host edge check; DA-54 to DA-58 are recorded. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M5-ADMIN-STEPUP-P1 keeps G-CPLOGIN, G-IAM, G-AUDIT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE and G-IDEMPOT `OPEN` and G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL`, and delivers the step-up experience in code ([03 §2a](./03-backend-control-plane-login-session-blueprint.md), [ADR-28](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** The existing admin login route gains an opt-in same-principal reauthentication mode (`X-TMPOS-Reauthenticate: 1`), and the console's Tenant Management page a recent-sign-in dialog that uses it and resumes nothing without a new confirmation, reason and key. That narrows G-CPLOGIN's step-up part and closes DA-34; no route, policy or shipping-route change is made, and no migration is added. Still open: the admin-host deployment and the real-edge check (DA-9), the login/logout audit record (G-AUDIT), the tenant boundary composition and the pre-operation security requirement (G-CPLOGIN); tenant- and store-scope authorization and D1 (G-IAM); the persistent runtime LOGIN and the owner-side provisioning (G-DBROLE); and any managed apply (G-MIGRATE). Nothing is deployed. DA-59 and DA-60 record the two M6-ENTRY-P1 findings for targeted pre-deployment follow-up; DA-61 and DA-62 record this stage's residuals. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M6-ADMIN-AUDIT-P1 keeps G-CPLOGIN and G-AUDIT `OPEN` ([03 §2b](./03-backend-control-plane-login-session-blueprint.md), [ADR-29](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** The administrative boundary's successful login, reauthentication and logout now write their `audit_event` row in the same transaction as the session change, in code only. No route, migration, grant or routine is added. DA-61 is rewritten, DA-35's decision is recorded, and DA-63 to DA-69 are new. Nothing is deployed. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M5-ADMIN-REVOKE-P1 keeps G-CPLOGIN, G-DBROLE and G-MIGRATE `OPEN` ([ADR-30](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** Every administrative change now takes effect when its transaction commits, except DA-76. The administrative login, the recent sign-in and the tenant-management commands are ordered against it in their own transactions, and migration 015 (one trigger function, three triggers, no grant) makes the changes 009 cannot lock wait. That implements G-CPLOGIN's pre-operation create condition and in-transaction re-verification for the administrative boundary, in code only. Its stamp-update clause is not implemented (updates are conditional on liveness only) and is an open owner decision (DA-77), and the requirement stays unmet for the tenant boundary. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–015, and D4 names the chain through 015. DA-61(b) is closed, DA-63 is resolved as a classification, DA-67 is unchanged, and DA-70 to DA-77 are new. Nothing is deployed. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M5-TENANT-SESSION-P1 keeps G-CPLOGIN, G-IAM, G-AUDIT, G-DBROLE and G-MIGRATE `OPEN` ([ADR-31](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** Every tenant-plane standing change now takes effect when its transaction commits. The tenant login and recent sign-in are one `establish` transaction that holds the actor's account, links, grants, tenants and stores and re-reads the standing, and the tenant login, recent sign-in and logout are audited. Migration 016 (one column, one table, two trigger functions, five triggers, two routines, two grants to `tmpos_app`) gives stores an access version, moves tenant and store versions on every status change and a store's on a move to another tenant, refuses any change of a tenant's or a store's key, starts every new store at 1, and counts every tenant- and store-scope grant change and every delete of a tenant or store such a grant names. That implements G-CPLOGIN's create condition for the tenant boundary in code; no entry composes that boundary, and no store route or permission is in production. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–016, and D4 names the chain through 016. DA-41 is closed in code, DA-61(b) is closed for the tenant boundary, DA-65 is delivered for the tenant audience, and DA-78 to DA-82 are new (DA-80 closed in code). Nothing is deployed. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P1 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE and G-CPLOGIN `OPEN` ([ADR-32](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It adds the first tenant business route in code, `GET /api/v1/customers`, under the first decidable tenant permission, `view_customers` (owner decision D1 for that route: the Customers domain at View or above, at tenant scope only), with the tenant taken only from the actor's one active tenant grant. Migration 017 adds schema `tmpos_customer`: the minimal customer identity (id, owning tenant, name), which no runtime role may read directly, and one definer read that re-confirms the named tenant grant in the same snapshot as a bounded keyset page; `tmpos_app` gets USAGE and EXECUTE only. The read is unaudited by owner decision. The route is served by no deployment, since no entry composes a tenant host. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–017, and D4 names the chain through 017. DA-39 is updated, and DA-83 to DA-92 are new (DA-89 to DA-91 record pre-existing or adjacent findings). Nothing is deployed. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P2 (commit 1, the database boundary) keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE and G-CPLOGIN `OPEN` ([ADR-33](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** Migration 018 makes every session the runtime creates go through a mint that requires, for a tenant session, an HMAC-SHA256 proof under an issuer key the runtime role cannot read, and it binds the customer read in the database to the presenting minted session, whose tenant the database derives. DA-83 is contained against the runtime credential alone, not closed. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–018, and D4 names the chain through 018. DA-66, DA-83 and DA-87 are updated, and DA-93 to DA-104 are new (DA-103 records a pre-existing finding). The tenant entry, which composes the key, is the stage's second commit. Nothing is deployed, and deployment is not authorized until the owner decides issuer-key custody and installs the key. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION `OPEN` ([ADR-34](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It adds the first tenant business command in code, `POST /api/v1/customers`. It runs under the second decidable tenant permission, `create_customers`: the owner's decision for that route, the Customers domain at Create or above, at tenant scope only, denied under `read_only`, with no role default changed. It is the first tenant-scope idempotent route, bound to the authorizer's trusted tenant and actor (contract version 2). It commits the customer, its audit record and the completion in one transaction, which holds the presenting session and the actor's standing and decides the permission again. Migration 019 adds schema `tmpos_customer_write` with one session-bound definer routine, and `tmpos_app` still holds no privilege on the customer table. The tenant entry now requires its own `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY`. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–019, and D4 names the chain through 019. DA-29, DA-83, DA-85, DA-87, DA-92, DA-93, DA-97, DA-101, DA-102 and DA-106 are updated, and DA-110 to DA-117 are new (DA-110 records a pre-existing driver finding). Nothing is deployed. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION `OPEN` ([ADR-35](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It makes both customer permissions follow an owner's edit, in code: migration 020 stores the Customers level of the three editable built-in roles per tenant, the store owner alone edits it through `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` (`edit_role_levels`, owner-only, with a recent sign-in, a reason, an audit record and tenant-scope idempotency; flagged for owner confirmation), and the authorizer and 020's routines decide `view_customers` and `create_customers` on it, and the revalidator decides the create again in its committing transaction (the read has none). `tmpos_app` no longer executes 019's create or 018's `m7a_customer_page_v2`. The shop app's editor is unchanged and does not reach the server. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–020, and D4 names the chain through 020. It touches G-IAM, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION, narrows DA-85, amends DA-97 and DA-115, and adds DA-118 to DA-124; DA-119 (a direct, unaudited role edit) is a new pre-deployment blocker and G-AUDIT exception. Nothing is deployed. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION `OPEN` ([ADR-36](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It connects the shop app's role screen to the tenant server for the built-in roles' Customers level, in code: the screen signs in through the M5 tenant-session login, reads the four built-in roles' levels through the new owner-only `GET /api/v1/roles/levels` (no recent sign-in; its tenant is the authorizer-selected trusted scope passed to 020's definer read, not bound to the session in the database) and changes `manager`, `technician` and `sales_staff` through R1's PUT, calling a change saved only after an exact 200 and a matching same-tenant read-back. The existing customer list and create, `GET` and `POST /api/v1/customers`, decide on the stored level. Custom roles and other domains stay local-only, and owner-only editing stays flagged for owner confirmation. The tenant entry now serves 9 routes: `/health`, `/readiness`, the session login, current session and logout, `GET` and `POST /api/v1/customers`, and `PUT` and `GET /api/v1/roles/levels`. There is no new migration: 001–020 and the four frozen operator paths (`scripts/supabase-migrate.ts`, `scripts/managed-baseline-launcher.mjs`, `scripts/managed-m005-launcher.mjs`, `server/platform-identity/migrationExecutor.ts`) are byte-identical, and the startup preflight still requires exactly 001–020. DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions. DA-43, DA-85, DA-107, DA-121 and DA-124 are amended, and DA-125 to DA-127 are new. A new CI step, "Shop role levels in a real browser against the tenant entry and disposable PostgreSQL (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2)", runs the browser gate in the runner's Chrome under the Firestore emulator (local runs use Chromium). Nothing is deployed, and no managed database, live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred. The next customer slice is `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}`. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 1 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION `OPEN` ([ADR-37](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It makes the owner's Customers level edit conditional, in code: `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` asserts the tenant and the per-role version of the read the owner reviewed, and migration 021 (schema `tmpos_role_edit`: a per-row version counter, a one-snapshot read of levels and versions, the conditional edit, and a trigger that advances the version on every update of a level row) judges both inside the committing transaction: the tenant and a preliminary version check unlocked, after the authority locks and before any role-row lock, then the authoritative version check under the role row FOR UPDATE. It writes nothing in either tenant when either fails. The refusal is an unsealed 409 `write_conflict`: no mutation, audit record, event or completion, and the separately committed idempotency lease stays until it expires. `tmpos_app` no longer executes 020's unconditional edit. The shop panel sends each draft's tenant and version, turns a stale draft into an explicit conflict, holds an unresolved attempt as its key plus its exact change until a 200 or a `write_conflict` settles it, and shows a change as saved only on an exact-match read-back. No route is added. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–021, and D4 names the chain through 021. 001–020 and the four frozen operator paths (`scripts/supabase-migrate.ts`, `scripts/managed-baseline-launcher.mjs`, `scripts/managed-m005-launcher.mjs`, `server/platform-identity/migrationExecutor.ts`) are byte-identical to the entry commit. DA-125 is closed for the built-in Customers server-backed editor; custom roles and other domains stay local-only. DA-119, DA-120, DA-121 and DA-122 are amended, and DA-128 to DA-130 are new. DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions. Two CI steps run the new database suites against the disposable service. Nothing is deployed, and no managed database, live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred. The customer detail read, `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}`, is the stage's second part and is not delivered here. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION `OPEN` ([ADR-38](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It adds `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}` to the tenant entry, in code:
- **Permission and path.** The route is under `view_customers`, and it is the route table's one template, admitted by name. *(Since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 the table admits exactly two templates by name; the second is `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}/profile`, [ADR-41](./10-architecture-decision-records.md).)*
- **Order of checks.** The chain's 401 and 403 come before the id is judged. Then a non-canonical id is a 400. A missing id and another tenant's id get the same status, error code, body shape and log reason, while per-request metadata differs. Their timing is not equalized, and that residual is recorded (DA-135).
- **Response.** The answer is `200 { customer: { id, name } }` only.
- **Database.** Migration 022 adds schema `tmpos_customer_detail` with one session-bound read routine that re-decides the list's authority on one snapshot. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–022, and D4 names the chain through 022.
- **Location.** The customer create's 201 now carries `Location`, sealed and replayed identically.
- **Tenant entry.** It serves ten routes. The administrative entry serves no detail path.
- **Register.** DA-131 to DA-136 are new. DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions.
- **CI.** One new step runs `tests/db/customerDetail.integration.test.mjs`.
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database, live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred.

**The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION `OPEN` ([ADR-39](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It gives the three customer routes their first shop client, in code:
- **The page.** A separate Customer directory page and sidebar entry in the shop app list the server's customers, open one by id and add one by name. Only an id and a name are shown. The Customers screen keeps its records, which are held only in the browser's memory and lost on reload, and gains a banner and a link; nothing is merged, imported, migrated or erased.
- **The session names the store.** The page uses the shop's existing tenant session and sends no tenant, store, role or actor. A changed session token clears what the page shows, reads again and detaches an unconfirmed create for good.
- **Reads.** A malformed success is a failure, never an empty list. A read the server refuses (a 401 or 403, or a sign-in it no longer accepts) clears both the list and the customer shown and never falls back to browser data. Any other failed read, a failed "Load more" included, keeps the rows already confirmed only under a "last known" label; a cursor the server no longer accepts reloads once from the first page instead. A missing id, another store's id and a malformed id show one not-found text.
- **Create.** One `Idempotency-Key` per submitted draft. Only a validated 201 is shown as saved; an undecided answer is held as unconfirmed and is re-sent only by the user, with the same key and bytes, on the same session. An answer that arrives after its page has gone is kept in memory and shown by the next page that holds a session.
- **No server change.** No route, permission or migration is added, and the tenant entry still serves ten routes.
- **G-PERSIST is not claimed.** These routes store only a customer's name, and nothing has been deployed or run against a managed database.
- **Register.** DA-137 to DA-146 are new, and DA-84 and DA-107 are amended. DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions.
- **CI.** The quality job's limit is now 30 minutes (it was 20). The browser step, renamed "Shop role levels and customer directory in a real browser against the tenant entry and disposable PostgreSQL (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2, M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5)", also runs the directory scenarios SCB-01 to SCB-06; its 12-minute step limit is unchanged.
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database, live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred.

**The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION `OPEN` ([ADR-40](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It corrects two of P5's recorded defects, in code:
- **Reads bound to the session.** `GET /api/v1/customers` (cursor pages included) and `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}` now require `X-TMPOS-Session-CSRF` equal to the presenting session's token. A missing, repeated, malformed or other session's token is 403 `forbidden`, after the cookie's 401 and before the authorizer or any read. The shop sends the token its session check just answered, and after a refused read it checks the session once.
- **Session reuse.** The tenant session 200s now carry `subject`, a labelled digest of the session's own verified principal. A page load reads the current session first and reuses it, with no login, when the subject is the signed-in user's. No session, or another identity's, signs in; an outage, a 429 or a malformed answer signs nothing in.
- **Unchanged.** The treatment of an unconfirmed create, idempotency, rate limits, permissions, the tenant entry's ten routes and migrations 001–022. No dependency, secret or endpoint is added; one existing transitive dependency is updated for a security advisory (below).
- **Dependency advisory (a separate, earlier commit).** Two `@grpc/grpc-js` advisories, GHSA-m9gg-hp2v-232j (High) and GHSA-f596-whhp-79r4 (Low), both fixed in 1.13.6 and 1.14.5 (published 2026-09-17), reached the advisory database on 2026-09-30, after P5's final runs, so the dependency audit would have failed for any commit. All three copies now resolve to one `@grpc/grpc-js@1.14.5` with its own `@grpc/proto-loader@0.8.1`; the Firestore client keeps `@grpc/proto-loader@0.7.15` from its declared range. The two under `google-gax` move from 1.14.4 within their declared ranges (`^1.10.9`, `^1.12.6`). The Firestore client SDK's copy moves by its `package.json` override from 1.9.16 to 1.14.5. **That override is outside `@firebase/firestore`'s declared range `~1.9.0`**: this is an out-of-range compatibility change, not a patch update, because no fixed 1.9.x release exists. The browser bundle does not include grpc-js. The override's selector is unversioned, as it already was for 1.9.16, so a later `@firebase/firestore` release would also receive 1.14.5 until the override is revisited. Firestore's client header still reports `grpc/1.9.15`; that is telemetry only. The compatibility evidence is the Firestore emulator suite, the shop browser gate and the admin console containment suite, all run on a clean install of the updated lockfile: Firestore emulator rules suite 41/41, shop browser gate 22/22, admin console containment suite 41/41 and the full verification, every step exit 0 except the raw `npm audit` exit, which the dependency-audit reconciliation (exit 0) decides. Production audit: 0 Critical and 0 High (before the change, 4 High packages, all from the one High advisory). The full tree keeps its 4 recorded development-only High packages. No manifest exception was added, and the audit thresholds are unchanged. Commit `df3fc4e`, exact-commit CI 36789768621 green on all 62 steps.
- **Register.** DA-138 and DA-143 are closed in code, and DA-137 is amended. DA-147 to DA-149 are new. DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions.
- **CI.** No workflow change. The existing steps run the new HTTP cases RB-01 to RB-06 (`tests/db/customerDirectory.integration.test.mjs`) and the browser scenarios SCB-07 and SCB-08 (`tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs`). Exact-commit CI: 36789768621 (`df3fc4e`) and 36791433899 (`5479d1e`), green on every step.
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database, live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred.

**The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY `OPEN` ([ADR-41](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It adds customer contact records and makes recovery after an ended session deliberate, in code:
- **Contact records.** Migration 023 adds a nullable `phone` and `email` and a database-written `created_at` to the server customer. Customers created before 023 keep NULL in all three; the migration's time is never written as a creation time (DA-152). The create takes an optional phone and email (bounded, trimmed, no control or Bidi_Control character; the phone needs a digit, the email exactly one `@` and no whitespace) and still answers `{ id, name }`. A name-only body keeps its bytes and fingerprint, and the idempotency contract version is unchanged.
- **The profile read.** `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}/profile` answers `{ id, name, phone, email, createdAt }` under the detail read's policy and order of refusals, the session binding included; a foreign and a missing id are the same 404. The router admits exactly two templates by name (DA-136, amended). The tenant entry serves eleven routes.
- **Database.** Schema `tmpos_customer_contact` holds the create and the profile read, each delegating its whole authority decision to 020's create or 022's read. `tmpos_app` gets USAGE and EXECUTE on them, loses EXECUTE on 020's create, and still holds no table privilege. The startup preflight requires exactly 001–023, and D4 names the chain through 023. The rollback refuses while any customer holds a phone, an email or a creation time.
- **The shop.** The directory page's add form takes an optional phone and email, and a customer's page shows them ("Not provided" when absent) with the creation time ("Not recorded" for a legacy customer). The Customers screen and its records are unchanged and not linked (DA-144, amended). This part is commit `0f957c3`.
- **Deliberate Reconnect.** Once the shop has confirmed this user's session in an open page, a 401 ends it there with no login: only the Reconnect buttons sign in again. A valid session of the same user is still reused, a fresh page still signs in on its first load (DA-150), and an unconfirmed create is never re-sent during recovery (DA-137 unchanged).
- **Register.** DA-150 to DA-155 are new; DA-118, DA-136, DA-144 and DA-147 are amended (none closed); DA-137, DA-148 and DA-149 are unchanged. DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions; synthetic evidence closes no gate.
- **CI.** One new step runs `tests/db/customerContact.integration.test.mjs`. Exact-commit CI: 36859957536 (`52f17d7`), green on attempt 2 (attempt 1 failed only M6R-PERF, the recorded rate-limiter load flake; that commit changes no server code), and 36873703631 (`77508c1`), green on attempt 1. 36877615589 (`0f957c3`), green on attempt 1.
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database, live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred.

**The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**
- **M0 carry-forward gates still open:** G-EMU, G-HIST, G-4USER.
- **M2's lockfile recovery is now proven on a CI runner.** Every push to `main` runs the lockfile-portability gate before a public-registry `npm ci`, then the typecheck contract and the typecheck ratchet (`ci.yml` quality job); all were green on `8883473d` (run 34779503875), the runner whose missing internal mirror caused the original failure. No M2 gate changes state here: moving G-EMU, G-APPSEC or G-COVERAGE is the owner's decision against each gate's own pass rule.
- **Go-live rule:** every `BLOCKER` must be `PASS` and every `HIGH` must be `PASS` or explicitly owner-waived (with documented risk) before G-GOLIVE can be requested.
- **Security-HIGH waiver rule:** waiving any **security** HIGH gate (e.g. G-APPSEC, G-WEBHARDEN, G-PROVIDER) requires, in addition to owner sign-off, (a) **Security sign-off**, (b) a **documented compensating control**, and (c) a **time-boxed remediation date**. This is why the previously-waivable security-integrity gates (webhook signature verification, the hardcoded PIN, the RLS-bypassing DB role, money-path idempotency) were **elevated to BLOCKER** rather than left owner-waivable.
- **Non-waivable carve-out — G-APPSEC's lockfile-portability subcriterion.** A G-APPSEC waiver may cover SAST/DAST/SCA **findings** (e.g. an open transitive High). It may **never** waive the pre-install lockfile-portability checker, which is **non-waivable and blocks merge independently of G-APPSEC's disposition**. Without this carve-out a subcriterion silently inherits its parent's waivability, and a waiver granted over an unrelated advisory would dissolve supply-chain lock integrity along with it — which nobody granting such a waiver intends. The carve-out is required because the usual "it would fail anyway" reasoning is **false here**: an unportable lockfile does **not** reliably break the build — npm *skips* an unfetchable `optional`/`devOptional` package and `npm ci` still exits 0. That silent path is the realised failure this gate exists to prevent (a green install that omitted `@types/react`), so the control cannot rest on the build failing loudly. Per the precedent above, a security-integrity control that must not be waivable is treated as blocking regardless of its host gate's severity.
- **Legal-HIGH waiver rule:** **G-PRIVACY is a statutory gate** (GDPR Arts 15–22 DSAR, CCPA/CPRA) — its absence at go-live is a legal violation, not a discretionary risk. Waiving G-PRIVACY (or its core export/deletion sub-scope) requires, beyond owner sign-off, (a) **legal-owner sign-off**, (b) a **documented lawful basis**, and (c) a **time-boxed remediation date** — an owner alone may not waive it. The core DSAR-fulfilment sub-scope is treated as effectively blocking for any deployment processing EU/CA-resident PII.
- **Severity semantics:** a gate's blocking severity may **exceed** the defect severity of its source gap (defect severity ≠ go-live-blocking severity). Examples: GAP-18 (defect High) → G-EMU (BLOCKER); GAP-24 (defect Medium) → G-HIST (HIGH). This is intentional, not an error.
- **Operational residual — carrier-credential rotation (owner action, not a code gate).** The eliminated Shipping sidecar accepted carrier credentials over unauthenticated routes and held them in plaintext process memory, and the preview surface that reached it was network-reachable. **This repository holds no evidence that any real carrier credential was ever entered, and none is asserted.** The residual is strictly conditional: **if** a real carrier credential was entered into the provider-settings surface at any time while the legacy preview/sidecar was reachable, **then** before production the store owner must (a) rotate that credential at the carrier/aggregator, and (b) review that provider account's billing and activity for the exposure window. The condition is **not decidable from repository evidence** — absence of historical misuse cannot be proven from this codebase, because the sidecar persisted no credential audit trail and its process memory is not recoverable. Accordingly this is an **owner operational production gate**, discharged by owner attestation, not by any test, scan, or code artifact in this repository; no local gate can close it and none claims to. **No email address, provider account, key fragment, credential identifier, or secret is recorded here, and none may be added.** Related code-side state: G-SECRETS is `PARTIAL` (the in-memory store is gone; encrypted-at-rest and rotatable durable secrets remain M6/M8).
- **Owner decisions RESOLVED (no open M1 owner decision remains):** **G-PCI — integrated payment capability is IN SCOPE** (semi-integrated/tokenized/provider-hosted; no raw cardholder data in TM POS2026; provider selected in M7b; PCI category validated before production). **G-PRIVACY — privacy & DSAR foundation is IN SCOPE** (implement, not exclude; distributed M1/M5/M6/M7/M8/M9). Both directions are **DECIDED**; both remain **evidence-based production gates** until their evidence passes.
